<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

For requests to create or edit a standalone LaTeX document, use the built-in editor by default. Create or edit the .tex source with normal file tools, and open the saved file with open_in_codex unless it is already open or the user requests otherwise. Keep follow-up edits in that same file and editor. Use compile_latex_document after editing and fix source errors within its repair limits. Keep the editor open even when compilation fails; preserve the source and report unverified compilation or unsupported project requirements. Discover these tools if deferred. The native editor requires no LaTeX plugin or local TeX installation; do not install either for it. Ordinary math explanations stay in chat.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies them into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

<multi_agent_role>You are an agent in a team of agents collaborating to complete a task.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

notification. This skill covers the full workflow from transcript review to guided drill recommendation, platform/browser QA, assignment, and notification verification.\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Guided Drill Assignment\\r\\n\\r\\nUse this skill to turn student performance context into a verified guided drill assignment in LSAT U.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe operating standard is: do not merely recommend a drill. Choose the right drill from the student\'s recent transcript/context, verify the drill actually launches and is wired correctly, assign it through the platform, then verify the student\'s notification surface.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Inputs\\r\\n\\r\\nExpect one or more of:\\r\\n\\r\\n- A student name or shorthand, such as `C`, `C. Alexander`, or another current student.\\r\\n- A request to inspect a recent transcript, chat export, meeting note, student page, or platform record.\\r\\n- A request to find an appropriate guided drill.\\r\\n- A request to assign a drill and make sure the student is notified.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the student identity is ambiguous and cannot be safely inferred from local context, ask one concise clarifying question before assigning anything.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Required Context\\r\\n\\r\\nFor LSAT U vault work, follow the vault operating order before making a recommendation:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Read `wiki/hot.md`.\\r\\n2. Read `projects/lsatu/README.md`.\\r\\n3. Read `wiki/dashboard.md`.\\r\\n4. Read the relevant student page under `students/`.\\r\\n5. Read the student\'s `assignment-log.md` if it exists so you do not repeat assigned work unless Joshua explicitly asks for a repeat.\\r\\n6. Read `wiki/guided-drill-verification-log.md` so already-verified drills are not re-QA\'d unnecessarily.\\r\\n7. Search all available transcripts, transcript downloads, chat exports, and meeting notes for that student.\\r\\n8. Sort candidate transcript/context files by the actual meeting date, not file modification time, and use the most up-to-date transcript as the primary source of truth.\\r\\n\\r\\nPrefer local sources first. Do not invent student weaknesses or assignment history. If an older transcript conflicts with a newer one, follow the newer transcript and mention the older file only as background.\\r\\n\\r\\nUseful searches:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nrg -n \\"student-name-or-shorthand|student full name|student email\\" wiki raw students projects\\r\\nfind students -path \\"*transcript*\\" -type f | rg -i \\"student-slug|student-name\\"\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Drill Selection\\r\\n\\r\\nChoose the drill by matching the student\'s most recent need to the drill\'s pedagogical purpose.\\r\\n\\r\\nEvaluate:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Newest transcript evidence by meeting date: missed concepts, confusion, pacing, task avoidance, question type weakness, or reasoning pattern weakness.\\r\\n- Student readiness: assign teach-first drills when the student needs instruction, not a cold test.\\r\\n- Drill metadata: title, slug, week/module, question type, tags, pool size, question count, estimated time.\\r\\n- Platform constraints: student must have classroom and drill access.\\r\\n- Assignment history: avoid assigning a drill already present in the student\'s `assignment-log.md` unless Joshua explicitly wants a repeat or the log says it should be repeated.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen the best drill is not obvious, produce a short ranked recommendation before assigning:\\r\\n\\r\\n```markdown\\r\\nRecommended: <drill title>\\r\\nReason: <one sentence tied to transcript evidence>\\r\\nBackup: <second drill title, only if meaningfully close>\\r\\nDo not assign yet because: <only include if blocked>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the user has already asked to assign and the best choice is clear, proceed without stopping.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Repo Checks\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore using a drill, verify that it exists in the app data and is wired to the platform.\\r\\n\\r\\nCommon files to inspect:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `data/guided-drills/_seed.json`\\r\\n- `src/data/guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`\\r\\n- `src/lib/guidedDrills.ts`\\r\\n- `src/pages/Drill.tsx`\\r\\n- `src/pages/teacher/desk/library/DeskLibraryGuidedDrillsSurface.tsx`\\r\\n- `src/lib/classroomNotify.ts`\\r\\n- `src/lib/assignmentEmail.ts`\\r\\n\\r\\nConfirm:\\r\\n\\r\\n- The drill slug and title exist.\\r\\n- The drill has enough eligible questions for the configured count.\\r\\n- The guided assignment flow creates `guided_drill_assignments`.\\r\\n- The classroom item is created from the assignment source row.\\r\\n- Completion writes `completed_at` and `status: completed`.\\r\\n- Notification code attempts inbox, bell, and email where applicable.\\r\\n\\r\\nRun targeted tests if the repo is available:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nnpm test -- --run src/lib/guidedDrillNavigation.test.ts src/lib/classroomItems.test.ts src/components/mainhub/TodayFocusPanel.test.ts\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nIf tests cannot be run, say why and continue with browser verification.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Browser Verification\\r\\n\\r\\nUse the in-app browser when available. The usual admin surface is:\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nhttps://lsatprep.study/teacher?ws=library&tab=guided-drills\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore doing full launch QA, check `wiki/guided-drill-verification-log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the selected drill is already listed as `verified-working`:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Do not repeat full preview/start-runner verification.\\r\\n2. Cite the existing verification log entry.\\r\\n3. Continue with student profile/access checks and assignment flow.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the selected drill is not yet verified, run this sequence:\\r\\n\\r\\nVerification sequence:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Open the teacher guided drills library.\\r\\n2. Search the exact drill title or slug.\\r\\n3. Confirm the visible card shows the expected title, module/week, question count, and tags.\\r\\n4. Open the preview.\\r\\n5. Confirm the launch card renders with the expected title, estimated time, and question count.\\r\\n6. Click `Start Drill`.\\r\\n7. Confirm the real drill runner loads with question text, answer choices, controls, and the expected URL parameters or question ids.\\r\\n8. Return to the guided drill library before assigning.\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not call the drill \\"perfect\\" unless the above checks passed. Prefer \\"verified to launch and load the runner\\" over broader claims.\\r\\n\\r\\nExisting noisy console warnings that do not block launch should be reported only if relevant.\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter a new drill passes browser verification, add it to `wiki/guided-drill-verification-log.md` with date, slug, evidence, and `verified-working` status.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Assignment Flow\\r\\n\\r\\nAssign through the platform, not by manually inserting rows, unless the user explicitly asks for a backend-only repair.\\r\\n\\r\\nTeacher UI sequence:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. On the exact drill card, click `Assign`.\\r\\n2. In the assign dialog, search/select the intended student only.\\r\\n3. Scope checks to the dialog so sidebar or to-do rail text does not create false positives.\\r\\n4. Confirm the dialog says exactly one selected student, unless the user requested multiple students.\\r\\n5. Click the primary button, usually `Assign to 1 student`.\\r\\n6. Wait for the success toast.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf there is any sign that the wrong student is selected, stop before clicking assign.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Post-Assignment Verification\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter assignment, verify live state rather than relying only on the toast.\\r\\n\\r\\nVerify:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Student identity and access flags.\\r\\n- Guided drill assignment row.\\r\\n- Classroom item row visible to the student.\\r\\n- Unread bell notification row.\\r\\n- Notification link points to the assignment route or classroom route.\\r\\n- Email leg, if available through logs or returned notification result.\\r\\n\\r\\nAfter verification, update the student\'s `assignment-log.md` with the drill, slug, reason, platform evidence, notification evidence, and current status. If the file does not exist, create it.\\r\\n\\r\\nExpected tables/objects:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `profiles`\\r\\n- `guided_drills`\\r\\n- `guided_drill_assignments`\\r\\n- `classroom_items`\\r\\n- `notifications`\\r\\n- `email_send_log`, if accessible\\r\\n\\r\\nProtect secrets:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Do not print auth tokens.\\r\\n- Do not print service-role keys.\\r\\n- Do not expose full student email addresses unless the user explicitly needs them.\\r\\n- Redact email recipients in final reports.\\r\\n\\r\\nExample final verification fields:\\r\\n\\r\\n```markdown\\r\\nAssignment:\\r\\n- student: <display name>\\r\\n- drill: <title>\\r\\n- assignment status: assigned\\r\\n- classroom item: visible_to_student true\\r\\n- bell notification: unread assignment notification exists\\r\\n- email: verified via log / returned OK by platform / not independently verified\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## Final Response\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep the final response short and operational.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse this shape:\\r\\n\\r\\n```markdown\\r\\nAssigned <drill title> to <student>.\\r\\n\\r\\nVerified:\\r\\n- <drill launch/browser check>\\r\\n- <assignment row/classroom visibility>\\r\\n- <notification evidence>\\r\\n- <tests, if run>\\r\\n\\r\\nEmail: <verified status or limitation>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nIf assignment was not completed, say exactly where it stopped and what is needed next.\\r\\n\\r\\nAvoid overstating. \\"Clear in-app notification exists\\" is acceptable only after verifying a notification row or student-facing UI evidence.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Example From Prior Workflow\\r\\n\\r\\nStudent: C. Alexander.\\r\\n\\r\\nSelected drill: `Necessary Assumption - First Taste`, slug `w2-07-necessary-assumption-first-taste`.\\r\\n\\r\\nReasoning: transcript context indicated Necessary Assumption needed a teach-first, calibrated drill. The drill is W2.7, 10 questions, 60 minutes, with Necessary Assumption plus Comparison/Causation tags.\\r\\n\\r\\nVerification performed:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Opened guided drill library.\\r\\n- Found the exact card.\\r\\n- Opened preview.\\r\\n- Started the drill.\\r\\n- Confirmed the runner loaded with 10 question ids and real question content.\\r\\n- Assigned to only C. Alexander through the dialog.\\r\\n- Verified the assignment row, classroom item, and unread bell notification.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse this as a pattern, not as a default assignment for every student.\\r\\n"}'}]

{'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"83e07a","wall_time_seconds":8.5740862,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3489,"output":"---\\r\\ntitle: Conditional Must Be True · Keep the Whole Statement\\r\\ntype: recap\\r\\nrecord_kind: primary\\r\\nstudent: yahunatan-zetvy\\r\\nsession_date: 2026-09-16\\r\\nsource: transcript-downloads/2026-09-16-yahunatan-zetvy-notes-by-gemini.md\\r\\nstatus: draft\\r\\nposted: false\\r\\nposted_at:\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Conditional Must Be True: Keep the Whole Statement\\r\\n\\r\\nOnly people willing to compromise should undergo mediation. This tells you what is required for mediation to be appropriate. It does not tell you who actually attends, or whether willingness alone is enough. Keeping those distinctions intact is the work of conditional reasoning.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Combine what the statements guarantee\\r\\n\\r\\nA Must Be True answer must follow from the information you were given. Sometimes you combine facts. Sometimes you connect rules. Identify what each sentence gives you before choosing a method.\\r\\n\\r\\nSuppose one type of flavonoid found in apples is an antioxidant, and antioxidants help prevent heart disease. You can conclude that at least one type of flavonoid found in apples helps prevent heart disease.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhy at least one? The first statement identifies one type with the relevant property. The second tells you what that property does. Together they support a claim about that type. They do not show that every flavonoid has the property, or that eating any apple prevents heart disease.\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep the strength of the conclusion equal to the strength of the evidence. Helps prevent does not mean always prevents.\\r\\n\\r\\nA rule works differently. If a person should undergo mediation, that person is willing to compromise tells you a relationship. It does not tell you that anyone actually should undergo mediation. A rule does not establish its own starting condition.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Check your reasoning\\r\\n\\r\\nAn apple flavonoid is an antioxidant, and antioxidants help prevent heart disease. Can you conclude that every apple flavonoid prevents heart disease? Explain.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Worked answer:** No. The evidence establishes the relevant property for at least one type, not every type. It says helps prevent, not always prevents. A second type without that property would fit the evidence and defeat the stronger answer.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Put the requirement on the right\\r\\n\\r\\nConsider this hypothetical rule: Only people willing to compromise should undergo mediation to resolve their conflict.\\r\\n\\r\\nMediation means trying to resolve a dispute with help from a neutral person. The rule limits who should use it. Willingness to compromise is required.\\r\\n\\r\\nFirst flatten the sentence into complete English:\\r\\n\\r\\nIf a person should undergo mediation, then that person is willing to compromise.\\r\\n\\r\\nNow abbreviate the complete ideas:\\r\\n\\r\\nM = the person should undergo mediation.\\r\\n\\r\\nW = the person is willing to compromise.\\r\\n\\r\\nM → W\\r\\n\\r\\nThe left side is sufficient: knowing M lets you conclude W. The right side is necessary: M cannot be true without W. Necessary does not mean enough by itself. A person can meet a requirement without qualifying for the activity.\\r\\n\\r\\nOnly if works the same way. Litigation should be pursued only if a person is sure their position is correct becomes: If the person should pursue litigation, then the person is sure their position is correct.\\r\\n\\r\\nL = the person should pursue litigation. C = the person is sure their position is correct.\\r\\n\\r\\nL → C\\r\\n\\r\\nBeing sure is required. It does not establish that the person should litigate. Also, being sure that a position is correct is not the same as the position actually being correct.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Check your reasoning\\r\\n\\r\\nOnly trained volunteers should lead the workshop. Write the complete if-then rule. Does training alone prove that a volunteer should lead?\\r\\n\\r\\n**Worked answer:** If a volunteer should lead the workshop, then that volunteer is trained. Training is required, but training alone does not prove the person should lead. Other considerations may matter. Reversing the rule would add a guarantee we were never given.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Keep should in the statement\\r\\n\\r\\nA recommendation and an action are different ideas. A person may do something they should not do. A person may also fail to do something they should do.\\r\\n\\r\\nM → W is about who should undergo mediation. It is not a rule about everyone who actually undergoes mediation.\\r\\n\\r\\nSuppose Alex actually attends mediation but refuses to compromise. That does not contradict this rule. Alex may be doing something Alex should not do. But if we say Alex should undergo mediation and Alex is unwilling to compromise, those two claims violate the rule.\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep words such as should, can, must, and actually when you translate. Removing one can change what the statement proves. An abbreviation saves writing only when you remember the whole idea behind it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Check your reasoning\\r\\n\\r\\nWe know only that Morgan actually underwent mediation. Does the rule prove that Morgan was willing to compromise? Why?\\r\\n\\r\\n**Worked answer:** No. The rule starts with should undergo mediation, not actually underwent mediation. Morgan could have attended despite being unwilling. The actual action does not activate this recommendation rule.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Reverse and negate to get the contrapositive\\r\\n\\r\\nThe rule M → W forbids one combination: M true with W false. If willingness is absent, the recommendation cannot be present.\\r\\n\\r\\nM → W\\r\\n\\r\\nnot W → not M\\r\\n\\r\\nIn English: If a person is unwilling to compromise, then that person should not undergo mediation.\\r\\n\\r\\nYou reverse the order and negate both complete ideas. The new statement describes the same restriction as the original. This is why it is valid.\\r\\n\\r\\nReversing without negating does something different: W → M. It says willingness guarantees that mediation is appropriate. The original rule never promised that. A willing person might have a conflict for which mediation is inappropriate for another reason.\\r\\n\\r\\nNegating without reversing is also invalid: not M → not W. A person who should not undergo mediation could still be willing to compromise.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe rule lets you move forward from M, or backward from not W. W alone and not M alone leave the other side open.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Test every possible combination\\r\\n\\r\\n| Should undergo mediation | Willing to compromise | Fits M → W? |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| True | True | Yes |\\r\\n| True | False | No |\\r\\n| False | True | Yes |\\r\\n| False | False | Yes |\\r\\n\\r\\nOnly M true with W false violates the rule. Both states with M false are allowed. Both states with W true are allowed.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Check your reasoning\\r\\n\\r\\nA volunteer is not trained. What follows from the workshop rule? What if the volunteer is trained instead?\\r\\n\\r\\n**Worked answer:** The untrained volunteer should not lead the workshop. That is the contrapositive: not trained → should not lead. A trained volunteer may or may not be someone who should lead. Affirming the requirement does not establish the trigger.\\r\\n\\r\\n## A statement about a whole group can be a rule\\r\\n\\r\\nSuppose a stimulus says: People whose conflicts are based on ideology are unwilling to compromise.\\r\\n\\r\\nIn this hypothetical stimulus, the statement applies to every person in the specified group. It is not a claim about all people, and it is not a general claim you should import into other questions.\\r\\n\\r\\nI = the person’s conflict is based on ideology.\\r\\n\\r\\nI → not W\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is a conditional relationship even without the word if. If a person belongs to the named group, the description applies to that person. It does not establish that any particular person belongs to that group.\\r\\n\\r\\nNow connect it to the contrapositive:\\r\\n\\r\\nI → not W → not M\\r\\n\\r\\nTherefore: People whose conflicts are based on ideology should not undergo mediation.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe middle idea matches exactly. The group rule gets you to unwilling to compromise. The contrapositive takes you from unwilling to compromise to should not undergo mediation.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the stimulus also says Lena’s conflict is based on ideology, that fact activates the chain. You can then conclude that Lena is unwilling to compromise and should not undergo mediation. Without that fact, your conclusion remains a rule about the group.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Check your reasoning\\r\\n\\r\\nUse I → not W and M → W. What must follow for anyone whose conflict is based on ideology? Does that prove someone actually has such a conflict?\\r\\n\\r\\n**Worked answer:** I → not M. Contrapose M → W to get not W → not M, then connect I → not W → not M. The conclusion is should not undergo mediation. The group rule does not establish that a member of that group actually exists.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Do not invent a choice between two activities\\r\\n\\r\\nThe separate rule L → C gives a requirement for litigation. It does not connect litigation to mediation.\\r\\n\\r\\nSomeone who should not undergo mediation does not automatically become someone who should pursue litigation. The stimulus has not said those are the only choices, or that every person must choose one.\\r\\n\\r\\nA counterexample makes the gap visible. Imagine a person who should use neither activity. That can fit both rules: the person does not have to meet the requirements for an activity they should not pursue.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe two requirements do not create an either-or choice. You need an additional statement connecting the activities before moving from one to the other.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Check your reasoning\\r\\n\\r\\nIf a person should not undergo mediation, must that person pursue litigation instead? Use a counterexample.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Worked answer:** No. The person could appropriately pursue neither activity. That violates neither requirement. The premises do not say that mediation and litigation are exhaustive choices, or that rejecting one requires the other.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Use the same process on a new question\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Identify what each sentence supplies: a specific fact or a general relationship.\\r\\n2. Translate each relationship into a complete if-then sentence. Preserve the subject, negation, and words such as should.\\r\\n3. Name the complete ideas before abbreviating. Put the required condition on the right.\\r\\n4. If you need the opposite direction, reverse and negate both ideas.\\r\\n5. Connect rules only when the ending of one matches the beginning of the next. Apply a fact only when it supplies a valid starting point.\\r\\n6. Read the result back in English. Test a tempting answer by trying to make the premises true and that answer false.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf you can build that counterexample, the answer is not guaranteed. If you cannot see a valid connection yet, do not add a fact or an extra choice to make the answer work.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Put the moves together\\r\\n\\r\\n1. A file may be released only if it has been reviewed. This file has not been reviewed. What follows?\\r\\n\\r\\n**Worked answer:** This file may not be released. May be released → reviewed; not reviewed → may not be released. Preserve permission: this does not prove that nobody actually releases it.\\r\\n\\r\\n2. Only licensed drivers may operate these vans. Sam is licensed. Must Sam be permitted to operate one?\\r\\n\\r\\n**Worked answer:** No. May operate → licensed. Licensing is necessary, but could be insufficient. Sam could be licensed and still lack permission for another reason.\\r\\n\\r\\n3. Every priority application needs supervisor approval. Approval is required only if the application is incomplete. What follows for priority applications?\\r\\n\\r\\n**Worked answer:** Priority → needs approval → incomplete. Every priority application is incomplete under these hypothetical rules. No particular priority application is asserted to exist.\\r\\n\\r\\nTransfer question: Only certified guides should lead night tours. All new guides are uncertified. Nora is a new guide. State the inference and one tempting answer that goes too far.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Worked answer:** New → not certified; should lead → certified; not certified → should not lead. Nora should not lead night tours. Nora never actually leads night tours goes too far: the rules concern who should lead, not what someone actually does.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Before the next session\\r\\n\\r\\nComplete the conditional-diagramming drills first, then move into the LSAT questions. The focus is translating and diagramming conditionals until the move becomes automatic.\\r\\n\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n## Notes for me (not posted)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Weak spots:** complete English before symbols; sufficient versus necessary direction; retaining should versus actual action; recognizing a universal group statement as a conditional rule.\\r\\n- **Follow-ups on my side:** Joshua promised conditional-diagramming drills, followed by LSAT questions (00:18:05–00:19:25, repeated at the close). Delivery is unverified. No question IDs, drill quantity, or deadline was specified.\\r\\n- **Watch:** Yahunatan found the required compromise condition and used the contrapositive. The next target is precise rephrasing that preserves modality, followed by automatic diagramming.\\r\\n- **Source conflict:** at 00:13:57 the transcript reads willing for the ideology group; at 00:16:23 and 00:17:13 both speakers clearly use unwilling/not willing. This draft follows the repeated negated reading. The original test stimulus is not attached, so exact original wording remains unverified.\\r\\n- **Source conflict:** the current teaching-notes shorthand says would not undergo mediation. The instruction at 00:11:52–00:12:58 explicitly distinguishes should from what people actually do. The draft preserves should not undergo; it does not silently reproduce that stronger action claim.\\r\\n- **Excluded:** travel and theological conversation remain in the source. No personal or religious claims appear in the student lesson. The ideological rule is labelled hypothetical, not a real-world characterization.\\r\\n- **Preview:** [[../../../brainstorms/2026-09-30-yahunatan-post-session-recap/conditional-reasoning.html]]\\r\\n- **Source map:** [[../../../brainstorms/2026-09-30-yahunatan-post-session-recap/source-map.md]]\\r\\n- **Related reference:** [[../../sofia/recaps/2026-09-11-recap]]; corpus Conditional Logic/Notes/_megadoc_notes_conditionals.md. This is a session-specific application of the existing conditional lesson, not a new course-wide source of truth.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"204618","wall_time_seconds":3.4555007,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":43,"output":"students/yahunatan-zetvy/live-status.generated.md:2:title: Yahunatan Zetvy Live Status\\nstudents/yahunatan-zetvy/live-status.generated.md:9:# Yahunatan Zetvy Live Status\\n"}'}]

The brain\'s assign_drill.py\\r\\n// shells out to it and does the writing.\\r\\n//\\r\\n// The draw itself is scripts/lib/drill-level.mjs, a port of the app\'s resolver\\r\\n// guarded by guidedDrillLevel.port.test.ts, so the set produced here is the set\\r\\n// the teacher UI would have produced.\\r\\n//\\r\\n// Usage:\\r\\n//   node scripts/resolve-drill-qids.mjs --slug <slug> --level beginner\\r\\n//     --count 15                 question count; omit for the drill\'s own\\r\\n//     --traps \\"Too Strong,Out Of Scope\\"\\r\\n//     --trap-weight 0.5          default 0.5 when traps are given, else 0\\r\\n//     --seed 12345               deterministic draw, for reproducing one\\r\\n//     --json                     machine-readable, nothing else on stdout\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { loadBank, loadSeed, loadStats, resolvePool } from \'./lib/drill-pool.mjs\';\\r\\nimport { resolveLeveledDrillQids } from \'./lib/drill-level.mjs\';\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction parseArgs(argv) {\\r\\n  const out = {};\\r\\n  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {\\r\\n    const a = argv[i];\\r\\n    if (a === \'--json\') out.json = true;\\r\\n    else if (a.startsWith(\'--\')) out[a.slice(2)] = argv[++i];\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return out;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst args = parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2));\\r\\nif (!args.slug) {\\r\\n  console.error(\'usage: resolve-drill-qids.mjs --slug <slug> [--level <band>] [--count n] [--traps \\"a,b\\"]\');\\r\\n  process.exit(1);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst LEVELS = [\'beginner\', \'intermediate\', \'advanced\'];\\r\\nconst level = args.level && args.level !== \'none\' ? args.level : null;\\r\\nif (level && !LEVELS.includes(level)) {\\r\\n  console.error(`--level must be one of ${LEVELS.join(\', \')} or none`);\\r\\n  process.exit(1);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst drill = loadSeed().find((d) => d.slug === args.slug);\\r\\nif (!drill) {\\r\\n  console.error(`no seeded drill with slug \\"${args.slug}\\"`);\\r\\n  process.exit(1);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst traps = (args.traps ?? \'\')\\r\\n  .split(\',\')\\r\\n  .map((t) => t.trim())\\r\\n  .filter(Boolean);\\r\\nconst trapWeight = args[\'trap-weight\'] != null ? Number(args[\'trap-weight\']) : traps.length ? 0.5 : 0;\\r\\n\\r\\n// Deterministic only when asked. The default draw is random on purpose: two\\r\\n// students at the same level should not get an identical set, and neither\\r\\n// should a re-attempt.\\r\\nfunction mulberry32(seed) {\\r\\n  let a = seed >>> 0;\\r\\n  return () => {\\r\\n    a = (a + 0x6d2b79f5) >>> 0;\\r\\n    let t = Math.imul(a ^ (a >>> 15), 1 | a);\\r\\n    t = (t + Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 7), 61 | t)) ^ t;\\r\\n    return ((t ^ (t >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\nconst rng = args.seed != null ? mulberry32(Number(args.seed)) : Math.random;\\r\\n\\r\\nconst bank = loadBank();\\r\\nconst pool = resolvePool(drill.bank_filter, bank);\\r\\nconst stats = loadStats();\\r\\nconst questionCount = args.count != null ? Number(args.count) : (drill.bank_filter?.count ?? drill.question_count ?? null);\\r\\n\\r\\nconst result = resolveLeveledDrillQids({\\r\\n  pool,\\r\\n  questionCount,\\r\\n  drillQids: drill.question_qids ?? null,\\r\\n  knownQids: new Set(bank.keys()),\\r\\n  level,\\r\\n  rng,\\r\\n  // Stats are always passed, targeting is not. With trapWeight at 0 the stats\\r\\n  // are inert for selection and used only to report what was drawn, which is\\r\\n  // the number Gate 1 is actually looking at. Anything else means a run\\r\\n  // without trap targets silently has no accuracy line.\\r\\n  stats,\\r\\n  targetTrapTags: traps,\\r\\n  trapWeight,\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\n// Reported alongside the draw because it is the number that decides whether\\r\\n// this drill is pitched right, and the caller should not have to join it back\\r\\n// to the level report by hand.\\r\\nconst payload = {\\r\\n  slug: drill.slug,\\r\\n  title: drill.title,\\r\\n  drillLevelTag: drill.level ?? Mirrors resolveGuidedDrillPool in src/lib/guidedDrills.ts.\\r\\n\\r\\nimport fs from \'node:fs\';\\r\\nimport path from \'node:path\';\\r\\n\\r\\nconst BANK_DIR = the local bank files know, keyed by qid. */\\r\\nexport function loadBank() {\\r\\n  const byQid = new Map();\\r\\n  for (const file of fs.readdirSync(BANK_DIR).filter((n) => /-LR\\\\.json$/.test(n))) {\\r\\n    let arr;\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      arr = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(BANK_DIR, file), \'utf8\'));\\r\\n    } catch {\\r\\n      continue;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    if byQid;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function loadSeed() {\\r\\n  return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(SEED, \'utf8\'));\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Cohort stats hydrated into the shape resolveLeveledDrillQids expects.\\r\\n * The sidecar\'s keys are abbreviated; this mirrors hydrate() in\\r\\n * src/lib/questionStats.ts.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function loadStats() {\\r\\n  if (!fs.existsSync(STATS)) return new Map();\\r\\n  const payload = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(STATS, \'utf8\'));\\r\\n  const map = new Map();\\r\\n  for (const [qid, raw] of Object.entries(payload.q ?? {})) {\\r\\n    map.set(qid, {\\r\\n      correctChoice: raw.c,\\r\\n      correctPct: raw.cp,\\r\\n      empiricalDifficulty: raw.ed,\\r\\n      topTrapLetter: raw.tl,\\r\\n      topTrapTag: raw.tt,\\r\\n      topTrapTagRaw: raw.ttr,\\r\\n      topTrapPct: raw.tp,\\r\\n      trapSpread: raw.ts,\\r\\n      choicePcts: raw.p,\\r\\n    });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return map;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Mirrors resolveGuidedDrillPool in src/lib/guidedDrills.ts. */\\r\\nexport function resolvePool(bankFilter, bank) {\\r\\n  const f = bankFilter ?? {};\\r\\n  const qtypes = new Set((f.qtypes || (qtypes.size && !qtypes.has((q.questionType || \'\').toLowerCase())) continue;\\r\\n    if (features.size && !(q.questionFeatures || []).some((x) => features.has((x || \'\').toLowerCase())))\\r\\n      continue;\\r\\n    if (reasoning.size && !(q.reasoningType || []).some((x) => reasoning.has((x || \'\').toLowerCase())))\\r\\n      continue;\\r\\n    const d = q.questionDifficulty;\\r\\n    if '{"chunk_id":"6e8208","wall_time_seconds":10.0062123,"session_id":96947,"original_token_count":583,"output":"/**\\r\\n * The proficiency band a student works at, and how it maps onto the question\\r\\n * bank\'s 1-5 difficulty scale.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * The bands overlap on purpose. Hard cutoffs (1-2 / 3 / 4-5) starve the middle\\r\\n * band: a drill narrowed to a single difficulty value runs out of questions on\\r\\n * any question type that is not one of the big four. Overlapping bands keep\\r\\n * every level drawing from a pool large enough to fill a full drill.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * This module deliberately has no imports. The bands are needed by the assign\\r\\n * path, the runtime resolver, the teacher UI and the tests, and anything that\\r\\n * imported the supabase client or the question bank singleton to reach them\\r\\n * would drag both into every one of those callers.\\r\\n */\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type StudentLevel = \'beginner\' | \'intermediate\' | \'advanced\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const STUDENT_LEVELS = [\'beginner\', \'intermediate\', \'advanced\'] as const;\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface DifficultyBand {\\r\\n  min: number;\\r\\n  max: number;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The one place the band numbers are defined in TypeScript. They appear in SQL\\r\\n * exactly once more, in the guided_drills.level backfill, and a spec asserts\\r\\n * that migration still agrees with this table.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport const LEVEL_BANDS: Record<StudentLevel, DifficultyBand> = {\\r\\n  beginner: { min: 1, max: 3 },\\r\\n  intermediate: { min: 2, max: 4 },\\r\\n  advanced: { min: 3, max: 5 },\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\nexport const LEVEL_LABELS: Record<StudentLevel, string> = {\\r\\n  beginner: \'Beginner\',\\r\\n  intermediate: \'Intermediate\',\\r\\n  advanced: \'Advanced\',\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\n/** The full difficulty range, used when no level applies. */\\r\\nexport const FULL_DIFFICULTY_RANGE: DifficultyBand = { min: 1, max: 5 };\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function isStudentLevel(value: unknown): value is StudentLevel {\\r\\n  return typeof value === \'string\' && (STUDENT_LEVELS as readonly string[]).includes(value);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Narrow an untrusted value (a DB column, a URL param) to a level.\\r\\n * Strict: \'Beginner\' and \'BEGINNER\' are not levels. The stored values are\\r\\n * lowercase by CHECK constraint, so anything else is a bug worth surfacing\\r\\n * rather than silently coercing.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function coerceStudentLevel<T>(value: unknown, fallback: T): StudentLevel | T {\\r\\n  return isStudentLevel(value) ? value : fallback;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function levelBand(level: StudentLevel): DifficultyBand {\\r\\n  return LEVEL_BANDS[level];\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

LOWER IS HARDER. */\\r\\n  meanCorrectPct: number | null;\\r\\n  /** How many of the returned questions lean on one of the target traps. */\\r\\n  trapMatches: number;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Fisher-Yates. The existing runtime used `sort(() => Math.random() - 0.5)`,\\r\\n * which is a biased shuffle and not seedable; this is neither.\\r\\n */\\r\\nfunction shuffle<T>(items: T[], rng: () => number): T[] {\\r\\n  const out = [...items];\\r\\n  for (let i = out.length - 1; i > 0; i--) {\\r\\n    const j = Math.floor(rng() * (i + 1));\\r\\n    [out[i], out[j]] = [out[j], out[i]];\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return out;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Drop unknown and duplicate qids, preserving the authored order. */\\r\\nfunction cleanQids(qids: readonly string[], knownQids?: Set<string>): string[] {\\r\\n  const seen = new Set<string>();\\r\\n  const out: string[] = [];\\r\\n  for (const qid of qids) {\\r\\n    if (!qid || seen.has(qid)) continue;\\r\\n    if (knownQids && knownQids.size > 0 && !knownQids.has(qid)) continue;\\r\\n    seen.add(qid);\\r\\n    out.push(qid);\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return out;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * What the returned set looks like in cohort terms. Reporting only: it never\\r\\n * changes which questions were chosen, so it is safe to compute on every path.\\r\\n */\\r\\nfunction describeSet(\\r\\n  qids: readonly string[],\\r\\n  stats: ReadonlyMap<string, QuestionStat> | null | undefined,\\r\\n  targets: readonly string[] | null | undefined,\\r\\n): Pick<LeveledDrillResolution, \'statsCoverage\' | \'meanCorrectPct\' | \'trapMatches\'> {\\r\\n  if (!stats || stats.size === 0) {\\r\\n    return { statsCoverage: null, meanCorrectPct: null, trapMatches: 0 };\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  let measured = 0;\\r\\n  let total = 0;\\r\\n  let trapMatches = 0;\\r\\n  for (const qid of qids) {\\r\\n    const stat = stats.get(qid);\\r\\n    if (!stat) continue;\\r\\n"}'}]

## assistant

Use custom catalog drills with exact `question_qids`, then the app’s `assignWork()`. No create/save guided-drill API exists in the app source; catalog insertion uses Supabase authenticated admin permissions. Assignment creation remains through `assignWork`, not manual assignment-row insertion.

Verified local pool:

```js
import { loadBank, resolvePool, loadStats } from './scripts/lib/drill-pool.mjs';

const bank = loadBank();
const pool = resolvePool({
  qtypes: ['Must Be True'],
  features: ['Conditional Diagramming'],
  difficulty_min: 1,
  difficulty_max: 3,
  count: 4,
}, bank).filter(q => !q.qid.startsWith('PT101-S3-'));
```

Pool has **14 questions**, five difficulty 2 and nine difficulty 3. Zero difficulty 1. Exact feature must be `Conditional Diagramming`; `Conditional` matches nothing. Exact qtype excludes MBT EXCEPT and fill-in variants.

Suggested unique progression, sorted partly by cohort accuracy:

| Date | QIDs | Difficulty | Mean accuracy |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-30 | PT137-S3-Q3, PT106-S1-Q14, PT138-S4-Q3, PT124-S3-Q19 | 2,2,2,2 | 84.75% |
| 2026-10-01 | PT130-S3-Q10, PT133-S1-Q6, PT148-S3-Q17, PT155-S1-Q15 | 2,3,3,3 | 79.5% |
| 2026-10-03 | PT138-S2-Q18, PT112-S1-Q14, PT139-S1-Q12, PT119-S4-Q22 | 3,3,3,3 | 72.25% |

No PT101.S3 overlap.

Minimal catalog payload:

```js
const { data: drill, error } = await supabase
  .from('guided_drills')
  .insert({
    slug: uniqueSlug,
    week: 0,
    order_in_week: dayOrder,
    title,
    category: 'Logical Reasoning',
    estimated_minutes: 20,
    question_count: 4,
    difficulty_min: 1,
    difficulty_max: dayMax,
    question_types: ['Must Be True'],
    question_features: ['Conditional Diagramming'],
    reasoning_types: [],
    start_button_label: 'Start Drill',
    instruction_card: { left_md: instructions, right_md: practiceSteps },
    bank_filter: {
      qtypes: ['Must Be True'],
      features: ['Conditional Diagramming'],
      difficulty_min: 1,
      difficulty_max: dayMax,
      count: 4,
    },
    question_qids: exactDailyQids,
    is_published: true,
    level: 'beginner',
  })
  .select('*')
  .single();
if (error) throw error;

const result = await assignWork({
  teacherId,
  studentIds: [studentId],
  item: { kind: 'guided_drill', drillId: drill.id },
  scheduledForLocalDate: day,
  questionCountOverride: 4,
  levelOverrides: { [studentId]: 'beginner' },
  overrides: { title, description, dueAt },
  notify: false, // if following root's draft-only notification choice
});
```

`question_qids` at drill level is a supported authored exact list and takes precedence over pool draws. Avoid `frozenQidsByStudent`: that path labels metadata `frozen_reason: artifact_attached`, which misstates this task.

Relevant files:

- `src/lib/guidedDrills.ts`: filter shape, pool resolution, exact-list precedence.
- `src/lib/guidedDrillLevel.ts`: exact lists survive level/count filtering.
- `src/lib/assignWork.ts:357`: supported assignment parameters.
- `scripts/lib/drill-pool.mjs`: raw bank parser and matching resolver.
- `src/lib/studentLevel.ts`: beginner permits difficulty 1–3.
- `supabase/migrations/20260507170553_452084b1-da25-48bf-a313-300e3063b333.sql`: catalog fields and authenticated admin RLS.

Recipe was checked against local code and bank only. No writes or browser QA performed.
